Russia's vast landmass stretches over the easternmost part of Europe and the northernmost part of Asia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249][^cite_note-natgeo-250][^cite_note-bite-251] It spans the northernmost edge of [Eurasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasia) and has the world's [fourth-longest coastline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_length_of_coastline), of over 37,653 km (23,396 mi).[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254] Russia lies between latitudes [41°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/41st_parallel_north) and [82° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/82nd_parallel_north), and longitudes [19° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_meridian_east) and [169° W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/169th_meridian_west), extending some 9,000 km (5,600 mi) east to west, and 2,500 to 4,000 km (1,600 to 2,500 mi) north to south.[^cite_note-255] Russia, by landmass, is larger than three continents,[^cite_note-256] and has the same surface area as [Pluto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluto).[^cite_note-257]&#x20;

Russia has nine major mountain ranges, and they are found along the [southernmost regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Russia), which share a significant portion of the [Caucasus Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caucasus_Mountains) (containing [Mount Elbrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Elbrus), which at 5,642 m (18,510 ft) is the [highest peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_elevation_extremes_by_region) in Russia and Europe);[^cite_note-cia-258] the [Altai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altai_Mountains) and [Sayan Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayan_Mountains) in [Siberia](29639.md); and in the [East Siberian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Siberian_Mountains) and the [Kamchatka Peninsula](678671.md) in the [Russian Far East](173135.md) (containing [Klyuchevskaya Sopka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klyuchevskaya_Sopka), which at 4,750 m (15,584 ft) is the highest [active volcano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_volcano) in Eurasia).[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] The [Ural Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ural_Mountains), running north to south through the country's west, are rich in mineral resources, and form the [traditional boundary between Europe and Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boundary_between_Europe_and_Asia).[^cite_note-urals-260] The [lowest point in Russia and Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_points_of_Europe#Elevation), is situated at the head of the Caspian Sea, where the [Caspian Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caspian_Depression) reaches some 29 metres (95.1 ft) below sea level.[^cite_note-261]&#x20;



Frozen [Lake Baikal](56865.md) near [Olkhon Island](1903623.md), the [third-largest lake island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_island#Lists_of_lake_islands) in the world

Russia, as one of the world's only three countries [bordering three oceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_bordering_on_two_or_more_oceans),[^cite_note-natgeo-250] has links with a great number of seas.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] Its major islands and archipelagos include [Novaya Zemlya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novaya_Zemlya), [Franz Josef Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Josef_Land), [Severnaya Zemlya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severnaya_Zemlya), the [New Siberian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Siberian_Islands), [Wrangel Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrangel_Island), the [Kuril Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuril_Islands) (four of which are [disputed with Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuril_Islands_dispute)), and [Sakhalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakhalin).[^cite_note-Arctic-263][^cite_note-264] The [Diomede Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diomede_Islands), administered by Russia and the United States, are just 3.8 km (2.4 mi) apart;[^cite_note-265] and [Kunashir Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunashir_Island) of the Kuril Islands is merely 20 km (12.4 mi) from [Hokkaido](58092.md), Japan.[^cite_note-Chapple-2019-2]&#x20;

Russia has one of the world's largest surface water resources and is second only to Brazil by [total renewable water resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_total_renewable_water_resources).[^cite_note-266] Its lakes contain approximately one-quarter of the world's liquid [fresh water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresh_water).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] [Lake Baikal](56865.md), the largest and most prominent among Russia's fresh water bodies, is the world's deepest, purest, oldest and most capacious fresh water lake, containing over one-fifth of the world's fresh surface water.[^cite_note-267] [Ladoga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ladoga) and [Onega](374540.md) in [northwestern Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Russia) are two of the [largest lakes in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_lakes_of_Europe).[^cite_note-natgeo-250] Russia has over 100,000 rivers;[^cite_note-natgeo-250] the [Volga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga) in western Russia, which is widely regarded as the national river, is the [longest river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_Europe#Rivers_of_Europe_by_length) in Europe and forms the [Volga Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga_Delta), the largest [river delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_delta) in the continent.[^cite_note-268] The Siberian rivers of [Ob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ob_River), [Yenisey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yenisey), [Lena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lena_River), and [Amur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amur_River) are among the world's [longest rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_by_length).[^cite_note-269]&#x20;

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Russia)

The size of Russia and the remoteness of many of its areas from the sea result in the dominance of the [humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) throughout most of the country, except for the tundra in the far north and the extreme southwest. Mountain ranges in the south and east obstruct the flow of warm air masses from the [Indian](14580.md) and Pacific oceans, while the [European Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Plain) spanning its west and north opens it to influence from the Atlantic and Arctic oceans.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] Most of northwest Russia and Siberia have a [subarctic climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subarctic_climate), with extremely severe winters in the inner regions of northeast Siberia (mostly [Sakha](57905.md), where the Northern [Pole of Cold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pole_of_Cold) is located with the record low temperature of −71.2 °C or −96.2 °F),[^cite_note-Arctic-263] and more moderate winters elsewhere. Russia's vast coastline along the Arctic Ocean and the [Russian Arctic islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Arctic_islands) have a [polar climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polar_climate).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249]&#x20;



[Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) of Russia

The coastal part of [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md) on the Black Sea, most notably [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi), and some coastal and interior strips of the [North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Caucasus) possess a [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) with mild and wet winters.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] In many regions of East Siberia and the Russian Far East, winter is dry compared to summer, while other parts of the country experience more even precipitation across seasons. Winter precipitation in most parts of the country usually falls as snow. The westernmost parts of Kaliningrad Oblast and some parts in the south of Krasnodar Krai and the North Caucasus have an [oceanic climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] The region along the Lower Volga and Caspian Sea coast, as well as some southernmost slivers of Siberia, possess a [semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate).[^cite_note-270]&#x20;

Throughout much of the territory, there are only two distinct seasons, winter and summer, as spring and autumn are usually brief.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998125–135Physical_Environment-249] The coldest month is January (February on the coastline); the warmest is usually July. Great ranges of temperature are typical. In winter, temperatures get colder both from south to north and from west to east. Summers can be quite hot, even in Siberia.[^cite_note-271] [Climate change in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Russia) is causing more frequent [wildfires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildfires_in_Russia),[^cite_note-272] and thawing the country's large expanse of [permafrost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permafrost).[^cite_note-273]&#x20;

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Wildlife of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Russia)

See also: [List of ecoregions in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ecoregions_in_Russia)

Russia, owing to its gigantic size, has diverse ecosystems, including [polar deserts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polar_desert), [tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tundra), [forest-tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest-tundra), [taiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiga), [mixed and broadleaf forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperate_broadleaf_and_mixed_forests), [forest steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_steppe), [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe), semi-desert, and [subtropics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subtropics).[^cite_note-climate-274][^cite_note-bite-251] About half of Russia's territory is forested,[^cite_note-cia-258] and it has the world's largest area of forest.[^cite_note-Gardiner-2021-275]&#x20;



[Yugyd Va National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugyd_Va_National_Park) in the [Komi Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komi_Republic) is the largest [national park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_park) in Europe.

[^cite_note-urals-260]

Russian biodiversity includes 12,500 species of [vascular plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vascular_plant), 2,200 species of [bryophytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryophyte), about 3,000 species of [lichens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lichen), 7,000–9,000 species of [algae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algae), and 20,000–25,000 species of fungi. Russian [fauna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna) is composed of [320 species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Russia) of [mammals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammals), over [732 species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Russia) of birds, 75 species of reptiles, about 30 species of [amphibians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibian), [343 species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_freshwater_fish_of_Russia) of [freshwater fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater_fish) (high [endemism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism)), approximately 1,500 species of [saltwater fishes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltwater_fish), 9 species of [cyclostomata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclostomata), and approximately 100–150,000 [invertebrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invertebrate) (high endemism).[^cite_note-climate-274][^cite_note-276] Approximately 1,100 rare and endangered plant and animal species are included in the [Russian Red Data Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Data_Book_of_the_Russian_Federation).[^cite_note-climate-274]&#x20;

Russia's natural ecosystems are conserved in nearly 15,000 specially protected natural territories of various statuses, occupying more than 10% of the country's total area.[^cite_note-climate-274] They include 45 [biosphere reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biosphere_reserve),[^cite_note-277] 64 [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_of_Russia), and 101 [nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zapovednik).[^cite_note-278] Although in decline, the country still has many ecosystems which are still considered [intact forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intact_forest_landscape), mainly in the northern taiga areas, and the subarctic tundra of Siberia.[^cite_note-279] Russia had a [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 9.02 in 2019, ranking 10th out of 172 countries, and the first ranked major nation globally.[^cite_note-280]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main article: [Politics of Russia](25704.md)



[Vladimir Putin](32817.md)\
[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Russian_Federation)



[Mikhail Mishustin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Mishustin)\
[Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_Russian_Federation)

Russia, by constitution, is a [symmetric federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symmetric_federalism) republic with a [semi-presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-presidential_system), wherein the [president](239646.md) is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state),[^cite_note-(Article_80,_§_1)-281] and the [prime minister](339344.md) is the [head of government](37570.md).[^cite_note-cia-258][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998383–428Chapter_7._Government_and_Politics-282] It is officially structured as a [multi-party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_system) [representative democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998383–428Chapter_7._Government_and_Politics-282] with the federal government composed of three branches:[^cite_note-DeRouen-2005-283]&#x20;

- Legislative: The [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) [Federal Assembly of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Assembly_\(Russia\)), made up of the 450-member [State Duma](1010187.md) and the 170-member [Federation Council](1268578.md),

  [^cite_note-DeRouen-2005-283]

  &#x20;adopts [federal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_law), [declares war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_war), approves treaties, has the [power of the purse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_of_the_purse) and the power of [impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_in_Russia) of the president.

  [^cite_note-constitution.ru.ch5-284]

- Executive: The president is the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Commander-in-Chief_of_the_Russian_Armed_Forces) of the [Armed Forces](25709.md), and appoints the [Government of Russia](9787373.md) (Cabinet) and other officers, who administer and enforce federal laws and policies.

  [^cite_note-(Article_80,_§_1)-281]

  &#x20;The president may issue [decrees of unlimited scope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decree_of_the_President_of_Russia), so long as they do not contradict the constitution or federal law.

  [^cite_note-285]

- [Judiciary](1838621.md): The [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Russia), [Supreme Court](5915064.md) and lower federal courts, whose judges are appointed by the Federation Council on the recommendation of the president,

  [^cite_note-DeRouen-2005-283]

  &#x20;interpret laws and can overturn laws they deem [unconstitutional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionality).

  [^cite_note-286]

The president is elected by popular vote for a six-year term and may be elected no more than twice.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-289] Ministries of the government are composed of the premier and his deputies, ministers, and selected other individuals; all are appointed by the president on the recommendation of the prime minister (whereas the appointment of the latter requires the consent of the State Duma). [United Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Russia) is the [dominant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominant-party_system#Eurasia) [political party in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Russia) and has been described as "[big tent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_tent)" and the "[party of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_power)".[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]&#x20;

### Political history



A chart of the political system in the modern Russian Federation

While there is scholarly debate over the significance of the *[veche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veche)* in the Middle Ages as a representative political body,[^cite_note-292] Russian society has historically been ruled by various forms of [autocracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autocracy).[^cite_note-u511-293] This pattern of a [centralised ruling system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centralized_government) has its roots in the late Middle Ages, with a unified Russian state being established as an [absolute monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_monarchy) in the late 15th century,[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] a governance structure that was followed until the early 20th century.[^cite_note-296] Following the 1917 Russian Revolution, the monarchy was abolished, and the Soviet Union was proclaimed as a [one-party](102459.md) [communist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_state) until its collapse.[^cite_note-u511-293] Brief periods of non-autocratic rule include the short-lived [Provisional Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Provisional_Government) in 1917 established during the [February Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_Revolution), and during the transition of post-Soviet Russia into a [flawed democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flawed_democracy) during the [presidency of Boris Yeltsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Boris_Yeltsin) in the 1990s.[^cite_note-w330-297][^cite_note-h185-298][^cite_note-Croissant-299]<sup>: 223 </sup>&#x20;

In the 21st century, following the [presidencies of Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_under_Vladimir_Putin) and [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Dmitry_Medvedev), Russia has experienced significant [democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding).[^cite_note-Croissant-299]<sup>: 223 </sup>[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] The political system evolved from [electoral authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_authoritarianism) into a consolidated [authoritarian regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian_regime).[^cite_note-Croissant-299]<sup>: 323 </sup>[^cite_note-t305-302] Some political scientists have characterised Putin as the head of a [dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship),[^cite_note-Kuzio-2016-303][^cite_note-Krzywdzinski-304][^cite_note-305] or a personalist regime.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307][^cite_note-t305-302] Putin's second tenure as president has led to further autocratization,[^cite_note-Croissant-299]<sup>: 512 </sup>[^cite_note-Mamaev-308]<sup>: 80–81 </sup> which has been the most significant since the Soviet era,[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] with some authors suggesting a regeneration of [totalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totalitarianism) elements.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Putin's ruling policies are generally referred to as [Putinism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putinism).[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

### Political divisions

Main article: [Political divisions of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_divisions_of_Russia)

Russia, by constitution, is a [symmetric federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symmetric_federalism) with the possibility of an asymmetric configuration. Unlike the Soviet [asymmetric model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymmetric_federalism) of the RSFSR, where only republics were "subjects of the federation", the current constitution raised the status of other regions to the level of republics and made all regions equal with the title "subject of the federation". The regions of Russia have reserved areas of competence, but regions do not have sovereignty, do not have the status of a sovereign state, do not have the right to indicate any sovereignty in their constitutions and do not have the right to secede from the country. The laws of the regions cannot contradict federal laws.[^cite_note-314]&#x20;

The [federal subjects](701438.md)[^cite_note-disputed-315] have equal representation—two delegates each—in the [Federation Council](1268578.md), the [upper house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_house) of the Federal Assembly.[^cite_note-constitution.ru.ch5-284] However, they differ in the degree of [autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_administrative_division) they enjoy.[^cite_note-316] The [federal districts of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_districts_of_Russia) were established by Putin in 2000 to facilitate central government control of the federal subjects.[^cite_note-317] Originally seven, currently there are eight federal districts, each headed by an envoy appointed by the president.[^cite_note-318]&#x20;



| Federal subjects | Governance |
| --- | --- |
| 46 [oblasts](646268.md) | The most common type of federal subject with a governor and locally elected legislature. Commonly named after their administrative centres.[^cite_note-319] |
| 22 [republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republics_of_Russia) | Each is nominally autonomous—home to a specific [ethnic minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Russia), and has its own constitution, language, and legislature, but is represented by the federal government in international affairs.[^cite_note-320] |
| 9 [krais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krais_of_Russia) | For all intents and purposes, krais are legally identical to oblasts. The title "krai" ("frontier" or "territory") is historic, related to geographic (frontier) position in a certain period of history. The current krais are not related to frontiers.[^cite_note-321] |
| 4 [autonomous okrugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_okrugs_of_Russia) | Occasionally referred to as "autonomous district", "autonomous area", and "autonomous region", each with a substantial or predominant ethnic minority.[^cite_note-322] |
| 3 [federal cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_cities_of_Russia) | Major cities that function as separate regions (Moscow and Saint Petersburg, as well as [Sevastopol](51587.md) in Russian-occupied Ukraine).[^cite_note-323] |
| 1 autonomous oblast | The only autonomous oblast is the [Jewish Autonomous Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Autonomous_Oblast).[^cite_note-324] |

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Russia)



&#x20;  Russia \
&#x20;   Countries on Russia's "[unfriendly countries and territories list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unfriendly_countries_and_territories_list)"\
The list includes Russia's geopolitical rivals—member states of the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_the_European_Union) and [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO) and their allies—that have imposed [sanctions against it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) for its invasion of Ukraine.

Russia has the [sixth-largest diplomatic network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_diplomatic_missions) in the world as of 2024. It maintains diplomatic relations with 187 [United Nations member states](31969.md), two [partially-recognised states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition),[^cite_note-325] and two [United Nations observer states](31969.md#Observers_and_non-members), along with [143 embassies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_embassies).[^cite_note-326] As the [legal successor of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succession_of_the_Soviet_Union),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998429–486Chapter_8._Foreign_Relations-327] Russia [retains its seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union_and_the_United_Nations) as a [permanent member of the United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council).[^cite_note-328] It is generally described by political analysts as a [great power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power).[^cite_note-gambit-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-l454-331][^cite_note-332] Russia is also a former [superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower) as the leading constituent of the former Soviet Union.[^cite_note-Reiman-2016-177] In the 21st century, many scholars view its global influence as being in decline.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-f495-334] Russia is a member state of the [G20](2206608.md), the [OSCE](22796.md), [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS), [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTO), and the [APEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation); and the leading member state of organisations such as the [CIS](36870.md),[^cite_note-335] the [EAEU](27290106.md),[^cite_note-336] the [CSTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_Security_Treaty_Organisation),[^cite_note-337] and the [SCO](152422.md).[^cite_note-338] It was also a member state of the [G8](59755.md) (now the [G7](1196634.md)) and part of the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_in_the_Council_of_Europe) before its expulsion from the two groups in 2014 and 2022, respectively.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]&#x20;

Russia maintains close relations [with neighbouring Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus%E2%80%93Russia_relations), which is a part of the [Union State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_State), a supranational confederation of the two states.[^cite_note-341] [Serbia](29265.md) has been [a historically close ally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Serbia_relations) of Russia, as both countries share a strong mutual cultural, ethnic, and religious affinity.[^cite_note-342] From the 21st century, relations between Russia and China have significantly [strengthened bilaterally and economically](30964882.md) due to shared political interests.[^cite_note-343] [India](14533.md) is the largest customer of Russian military equipment, and the two countries share [a strong strategic and diplomatic relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Russia_relations) since the Soviet era.[^cite_note-344] Russia wields significant political influence across the [geopolitically](163225.md) important [South Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus) and Central Asia,[^cite_note-345] described in Russia as the "[near abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_abroad)",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998429–486Chapter_8._Foreign_Relations-327][^cite_note-Kolstø-346] while foreign political analysts have described the two regions as being part of Russia's "backyard".[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348]&#x20;

Russia shares [a complex strategic, energy, and defence relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) with [Turkey](11125639.md).[^cite_note-349] It maintains [cordial relations](3912038.md) with [Iran](14653.md), as it is a strategic and economic ally.[^cite_note-350] Russia has also significantly developed [its relations with North Korea](11438091.md) following its invasion of Ukraine in 2022, with increased defence co-operation.[^cite_note-351] At the same time, its relations [with neighbouring Ukraine](17446303.md) and the Western world—specifically the [United States](4482509.md) and the countries of the [European Union](3666797.md) and [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO%E2%80%93Russia_relations)—have collapsed.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353]&#x20;



Putin with G20 counterparts in [Osaka](52230.md), 2019

In the 21st century, Russia has pursued an aggressive foreign policy aimed at securing [regional dominance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_power) in Europe and increasing its international influence, as well as increasing domestic support for the government. It has initiated military interventions in the [post-Soviet states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Soviet_states) of Georgia and Ukraine, [as well as in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war) during [its prolonged civil war](30741795.md) in a bid to increase its influence in the [Middle East](19323.md),[^cite_note-354] and achieve its great power ambitions.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] Two-thirds of the global population, specifically the [developing countries](78449.md) of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_South), are either neutral or leaning towards Russia politically.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] Russian state-funded [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group) has been [deployed in Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group_activities_in_Africa) ([Africa Corps](78298390.md)),[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] and [in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group_activities_in_Syria);[^cite_note-361] to maintain political stability in [failed states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Failed_state) through military projection and exploit local natural resources.[^cite_note-362] Russia has also increasingly pushed to expand its influence across the [Arctic](36971.md),[^cite_note-363] the [Asia–Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia%E2%80%93Pacific),[^cite_note-364] and [Latin America](18524.md).[^cite_note-365] It has also continued using subversive tactics in its rival countries to project its geopolitical power,[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-gambit-329] such as [cyberwarfare](21093198.md), [disinformation campaigns](70210841.md),[^cite_note-367] [sabotage](29462.md) attacks,[^cite_note-368] [assassination attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_assassinations),[^cite_note-369] [airspace violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violations_of_non-combatant_airspaces_during_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine),[^cite_note-370] electoral interferences,[^cite_note-371] nuclear sabre-rattling,[^cite_note-372] and [hybrid warfare](78521073.md).[^cite_note-r890-373]&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md)



[Sukhoi Su-57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Su-57), a [fifth-generation fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth-generation_fighter) of the [Russian Air Force](1526308.md)

[^cite_note-374]

The Russian Armed Forces are divided into the [Ground Forces](1094752.md), the [Navy](383258.md), and the [Aerospace Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Aerospace_Force)—and there are also two independent arms of service: the [Strategic Missile Troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_Missile_Troops) and the [Airborne Troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Airborne_Troops).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998487–552Chapter_9._The_Armed_Forces-375][^cite_note-cia-258] As of 2025, the military have 1.1 million active-duty personnel, which is the world's [fifth-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel), and about 1.5 million [reserve personnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_reserve_force).[^cite_note-376] It is mandatory for all male citizens aged 18–27 to be [drafted](5735.md) for a year of service in the Armed Forces.[^cite_note-cia-258]&#x20;

Russia is among the five [recognised](22107.md) [nuclear-weapons states](400821.md), with the world's largest stockpile of nuclear weapons; over half of the world's nuclear weapons are [owned by Russia](588941.md).[^cite_note-377] Russia possesses the second-largest fleet of [ballistic missile submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic_missile_submarine),[^cite_note-378] and is one of the only three countries operating [strategic bombers](214209.md).[^cite_note-379] As of 2025, Russia maintains the world's [third-highest military expenditure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_military_expenditures), spending \$190 billion, corresponding to about 7.5% of its GDP.***[^cite_note-SIPRI-2020-380]*** It was also the [third-largest arms exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_arms_exports) in 2020–2024,[^cite_note-381] and has [a large and indigenous defence industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Russia), which produces the majority of its military equipment.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]&#x20;

### Law, corruption and crime

Main articles: [Law of Russia](528163.md), [Law enforcement in Russia](11137255.md), [Crime in Russia](15363104.md), and [Corruption in Russia](27352290.md)

Post-Soviet Russia under the regime of Vladimir Putin has been governed by a form of [crony capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crony_capitalism).[^cite_note-crony-385][^cite_note-386] Its political system has been variously described as a [kleptocracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kleptocracy),[^cite_note-387] an [oligarchy](22315.md),[^cite_note-388] and a [plutocracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plutocracy).[^cite_note-crony-385] As of 2025, it is the lowest rated European country in [Transparency International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transparency_International)'s annual [Corruption Perceptions Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Perceptions_Index), ranking 157th out of the 180 countries listed.[^cite_note-389]&#x20;



Opposition leader [Alexei Navalny](29174356.md) leading protestors in Moscow in the nationwide [anti-corruption protests of 2017–2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%932018_Russian_protests)

Corruption has significantly increased following the collapse of the Soviet Union,[^cite_note-SuharaManabu-390] and is seen as a significant issue in society.[^cite_note-markus-391][^cite_note-392] It affects various sectors, including the economy,[^cite_note-markus-391] the [government](9787373.md),[^cite_note-SuharaManabu-390] [law enforcement](11137255.md),[^cite_note-393] [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Russia),[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] [education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Russia),[^cite_note-396] and the military.[^cite_note-397] Russia's [shadow economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Informal_economy) was estimated to be about 44% of the total GDP in 2018.[^cite_note-398] [Penal military units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_penal_military_units_during_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) have been deployed as [storm troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_troops) during the ongoing Russo-Ukrainian War since 2022, such as the [Storm-Z](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storm-Z) and [Storm-V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storm-V) units.[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] According to estimates by the BBC, around 48,000 prisoners were recruited to fight for the Wagner Group.[^cite_note-401]&#x20;

The primary and fundamental statement of laws in Russia is the constitution. Statutes, such as the [Russian Civil Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Civil_Code) and the [Russian Criminal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Criminal_Code), are the predominant legal sources of Russian law.[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403] Russia has the [largest incarcerated population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_incarceration_rate) in Europe, and the fifth-largest incarcerated population in the world.[^cite_note-icjpr-404] Its incarceration rate is among the highest in Europe,[^cite_note-405] although the number has fallen steadily, by 59% since 2000.[^cite_note-icjpr-404] As of 2021, Russia's [intentional homicide rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_intentional_homicide_rate) stood at 6.8 per 100,000 people.[^cite_note-406] In 2023, Russia had the world's second-largest illegal arms trade market, after the United States, was described as a key hub for human trafficking, and was ranked first in Europe and 19th globally in the [Global Organized Crime Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Organized_Crime_Index).[^cite_note-407]&#x20;

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in Russia](785595.md)

Violations of human rights in Russia have been increasingly reported by leading democracy and [human rights groups](30335636.md). In particular, [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) say that Russia is not democratic and allows few political rights and civil liberties to its citizens.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]&#x20;

Since 2004, [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) has ranked Russia as "not free" in its *[Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World)* survey.[^cite_note-410] Since 2011, the [Economist Intelligence Unit](15303116.md) has ranked Russia as an "authoritarian regime" in its [Democracy Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_Index), ranking it 148th out of 167 countries in 2025.[^cite_note-411] In regards to [media freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_freedom_in_Russia), Russia was ranked 172th out of 180 countries in [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md)' [Press Freedom Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index) for 2026.[^cite_note-412] The Russian government has been widely criticised by political dissidents and [human rights activists](4358082.md) for [unfair elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Russia),[^cite_note-413] crackdowns on [opposition political parties and protests](38212364.md),[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415] [persecution of non-governmental organisations](47211692.md) and enforced suppression and [killings of independent journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_journalists_killed_in_Russia),[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418] and [censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_the_Russian_Federation) of mass media and [internet](37529240.md).[^cite_note-419]&#x20;



Following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), [anti-war protests](70159281.md) broke out across Russia. The protests have been met with widespread repression, leading to about 15,000 people being arrested.

[^cite_note-420]

Muslims, especially [Salafis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi_movement), have faced persecution in Russia.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] To quash the [insurgency in the North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurgency_in_the_North_Caucasus), Russian authorities have been accused of indiscriminate killings,[^cite_note-423] arrests, forced disappearances, and torture of civilians.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] In [Dagestan](751099.md), some Salafis along with facing government harassment based on their appearance, have had their homes blown up in counterinsurgency operations.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] Chechens and [Ingush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingush_people) in [Russian prisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_prisons) reportedly take more abuse than other ethnic groups.[^cite_note-428] During the 2022 invasion of Ukraine, Russia has set up [filtration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_filtration_camps_for_Ukrainians) where many Ukrainians are subjected to abuses and forcibly sent to Russia; the camps have been compared to [those used in the Chechen Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filtration_camp_system_in_Chechnya).[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] Political repression also increased following the start of the invasion, with [laws adopted](70646883.md) that establish punishments for "discrediting" the armed forces.[^cite_note-431]&#x20;

Russia has introduced several restrictions on [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Russia). In 2013, [an anti-LGBTQ law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_anti-LGBTQ_law) banning "gay propaganda" was unanimously passed by the State Duma and the Federation Council, later being signed into law by Vladimir Putin.[^cite_note-432] In 2020, the Russian parliament legalised a constitutional ban on [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Russia),[^cite_note-433] and in 2021 the [Ministry of Justice](35320735.md) designated the LGBTQ rights group [Russian LGBT Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_LGBT_Network) as a "[foreign agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_agent_\(Russia\))".[^cite_note-434] In 2022, further amendments were made to the 2013 anti-LGBTQ law.[^cite_note-435] In 2023, the Russian parliament passed a bill banning [gender reassignment surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_reassignment_surgery) for [transgender](19904525.md) people and the [Supreme Court of Russia](5915064.md) banned the [international LGBTQ movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_movements) as "extremist", outlawing it in the country.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437] In 2024, the Supreme Court issued the first convictions from the latter ruling.[^cite_note-438]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Russia](25705.md)

Further information: [Economic history of the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_history_of_the_Russian_Federation) and [Taxation in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Russia)

Russia has a [high-income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy),[^cite_note-439] industrialised,[^cite_note-440] [mixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy) [market-oriented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy) economy following a [turbulent transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_therapy_\(economics\)) from the [Soviet planned model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy) during the 1990s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998295–382The_Economy-441][^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] According to the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md), it has the [ninth-largest economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) by nominal GDP and the [fourth-largest economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) by GDP ([PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)).[^cite_note-IMFWEO.RU-10] As of 2023, the [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy) accounts for roughly 57% of total GDP, followed by the [industrial sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_sector) (30%), while the [agricultural sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_sector) is the smallest, at 3% of total GDP.[^cite_note-cia-258] It has a labour force of about 72 million, which is the [ninth-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_labour_force) in the world.[^cite_note-445] [Russia's largest trading partner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_trading_partners_of_Russia) is China.[^cite_note-oec-446]&#x20;



The [Moscow International Business Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_International_Business_Center)

Russia's [human development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_development_\(economics\)) is [ranked as](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index) "very high" in the annual [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index).[^cite_note-447] Roughly 70% of Russia's total GDP is driven by [final consumption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Final_consumption_expenditure),[^cite_note-448] and the country has the world's [twelfth-largest consumer market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_consumer_markets).[^cite_note-449] According to *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)*, Russia has the [fifth-highest number of billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_billionaires) in the world.[^cite_note-450] However, its [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_income_equality) remains high compared to other developed countries.[^cite_note-451] The variance of natural resources among its federal subjects has also led to [regional economic disparities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_federal_subjects_of_Russia_by_GDP_per_capita).[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] High [levels of corruption](27352290.md),[^cite_note-454] declining oil export revenues,[^cite_note-455] a shrinking labour force,[^cite_note-456] [human capital flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_capital_flight),[^cite_note-457] and an [aging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aging_of_Russia) and [declining population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Russia) also remain major barriers to future economic growth.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459]&#x20;

Following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), the country has faced [extensive sanctions](70323386.md) and other negative financial actions from the [Western world](21208200.md) and its allies which have the aim of isolating the Russian economy from the Western financial system.[^cite_note-Walsh-2022-239] However, Russia has completed its transition into a [war economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_economy),[^cite_note-460] and has shown resilience to such measures broadly, maintaining economic stability and growth—driven primarily by high [military expenditure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_with_highest_military_expenditures),[^cite_note-461] rising [household consumption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_final_consumption_expenditure) and [wages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_federal_subjects_by_average_wage),[^cite_note-462] low [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment),[^cite_note-reutersunem-463] and increased [government spending](1203070.md).[^cite_note-464] Yet, [inflation](38286.md) has remained comparatively high,[^cite_note-465] with experts predicting the sanctions will have a long-term negative effect on the Russian economy.[^cite_note-466] Additionally, the international sanctions have led Russia to become heavily economically and technologically dependent on China.[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-\:152-468]&#x20;

### Transport and energy

Main articles: [Transport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Russia) and [Energy in Russia](14049009.md)

[Railway transport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Russia) is mostly controlled by the state-run [Russian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Railways). The total length of common-used railway tracks is the world's [third-longest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size), exceeding 87,000 km (54,100 mi).[^cite_note-469] As of 2019, Russia has the world's [fifth-largest road network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_road_network_size), with over 1.5 million km of roads.[^cite_note-470] However, its road density is among the world's lowest, in part to its vast land area.[^cite_note-471] Russia's inland waterways are the [longest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_waterways_length), totalling 102,000 km (63,380 mi).[^cite_note-472] It has over [900 airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Russia),[^cite_note-473] ranking seventh in the world, of which the [busiest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Russia) is [Sheremetyevo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheremetyevo_International_Airport) in Moscow. The largest ports include the [Port of Novorossiysk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Novorossiysk), the [Great Port of Saint Petersburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Port_of_Saint_Petersburg) and the [Port of Vladivostok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Vladivostok).[^cite_note-474]&#x20;



The [Trans-Siberian Railway](56912.md) is the longest railway line in the world, connecting Moscow to [Vladivostok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladivostok).[^cite_note-475]



[Sheremetyevo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheremetyevo_International_Airport) is the [busiest airport in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Russia).

Russia has one of the world's largest amounts of [energy resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_energy_resources) throughout its vast landmass, particularly [natural gas](22131.md) and [oil](63420573.md), which play a crucial role in its energy self-sufficiency and exports.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998295–382The_Economy-441] It has been widely described [as an energy superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_as_an_energy_superpower).[^cite_note-476] Russia has the world's largest [proven gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves),[^cite_note-477] the second-largest [coal reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_coal_reserves),[^cite_note-478] the eighth-largest [proven oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves),[^cite_note-479] and the largest [oil shale reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale_reserves) in Europe.[^cite_note-480] As of 2023, it is also the [second-largest producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_production)[^cite_note-481] and the [third-largest exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_exports) of natural gas,[^cite_note-482] as well as the second-largest [producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_oil_production) and [exporter](17808102.md) of [crude oil](23195.md).[^cite_note-483] Russia's large oil and gas sector accounted for 30% of its federal budget revenues in 2024, down from 50% in the mid-2010s, suggesting economic diversification.[^cite_note-484]&#x20;

Russia is the world's third-largest energy producer as of 2023.[^cite_note-485] [Fossil fuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) account for over 64% of energy production and 87% of energy consumption.[^cite_note-energy-486] Natural gas is by far the largest source of energy, comprising over half of the energy production and 42% of electricity consumption.[^cite_note-energy-486] Russia was the first country to develop civilian nuclear power, building the world's [first nuclear power plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obninsk_Nuclear_Power_Plant) in 1954, and remains a pioneer in nuclear energy technology and is considered a world leader in [fast neutron reactors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast-neutron_reactor).[^cite_note-\:4-487] Russia is the world's [fourth-largest nuclear energy producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_by_country). Russian energy policy aims to expand the role of nuclear energy and develop new reactor technology.[^cite_note-\:4-487] Russia is the sole country that builds and operates [nuclear-powered icebreakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear-powered_icebreaker),[^cite_note-icebreaker-488] which ease navigation along the [Northern Sea Route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Sea_Route),[^cite_note-icebreaker-488]<sup>: 192 </sup> and aid in utilising its [Arctic policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_policy_of_Russia) in its [continental shelf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_shelf_of_Russia).[^cite_note-489]&#x20;

Russia joined the [Paris Agreement](30242372.md) on [climate change](5042951.md) in 2015, and ratified the agreement in 2019.[^cite_note-490] [Its greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_Russia) are the [fourth-largest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_greenhouse_gas_emissions) as of 2023.[^cite_note-491] Coal accounts for over 10% of its energy consumption.[^cite_note-energy-486] Russia is the [fifth-largest hydroelectric producer](381399.md#World_hydroelectric_capacity) as of 2022,[^cite_note-492] with hydroelectric power contributing almost a fifth to the total energy generation (17%).[^cite_note-energy-486] Though it is the [eighth-largest renewable energy producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_renewable_electricity_production) as of 2023, the use and development of other [renewable energy](25784.md) resources remain negligible,[^cite_note-energy-486] as Russia is among the few countries without strong governmental or public support for a [renewable energy transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_transition).[^cite_note-\:5-493]&#x20;

### Agriculture and fishery

Main articles: [Agriculture in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Russia) and [Fishing industry in Russia](20919132.md)



Wheat in [Tomsk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomsk_Oblast), Siberia

Agriculture, [forestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forestry_in_Russia) and [fishing](20919132.md) contributes about 3.3% of the country's total GDP as of 2023.[^cite_note-494] It has the world's [fourth-largest cultivated area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_use_statistics_by_country), at 1,265,267 square kilometres (488,522 sq mi). However, due to the harshness of its environment, only about 13.1% of its land is [agricultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_land),[^cite_note-cia-258] with an additional 7.4% being [arable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land).[^cite_note-495] The country's agricultural land is considered part of the "[breadbasket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breadbasket)" of Europe.[^cite_note-496] More than one-third of the sown area is devoted to fodder crops, and the remaining farmland is used [industrial crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonfood_crop), vegetables, and fruits.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-497] The main product of Russian farming has always been grain, which occupies well over half the cropland.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-497] Russia is the world's [largest exporter of wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_wheat_exports) and the [largest producer of barley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_barley_production) and [buckwheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_producing_countries_of_agricultural_commodities).[^cite_note-oec-446][^cite_note-fao-498] It is also among the largest exporters of [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) and [sunflower oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunflower_oil), as well as the leading producer of [fertiliser](37401.md).[^cite_note-fao-498][^cite_note-oec-446]&#x20;

Various analysts of [climate change adaptation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_adaptation) foresee large opportunities for Russian agriculture during the rest of the 21st century as arability increases in Siberia, which would lead to both internal and external migration to the region.[^cite_note-499] Owing to its large coastline along three oceans and twelve marginal seas, Russia maintains the world's [sixth-largest fishing industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing_industry_by_country), capturing nearly 5 million tons of fish in 2018.[^cite_note-500] It is home to the world's finest caviar, the [beluga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beluga_\(sturgeon\)), and produces about one-third of all canned fish and some one-fourth of the world's total fresh and frozen fish.[^cite_note-agriculturebritannica-497]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Russia)

See also: [Timeline of Russian innovation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Russian_innovation), [List of Russian scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_scientists), and [List of Russian inventors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_inventors)

Russia spent about 1% of its GDP on [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) in 2019, with the world's [tenth-highest budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_research_and_development_spending).[^cite_note-501] It also ranked tenth worldwide in the number of scientific publications in 2020, with roughly 1.3 million papers.[^cite_note-502] Since 1904, [Nobel Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nobel_laureates_by_country) were awarded to 26 Soviets and Russians in [physics](52497.md), [chemistry](25416893.md), [medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_medicine), [economy](23535306.md), [literature](23385442.md) and [peace](26230922.md).[^cite_note-503] Russia ranked 60th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506]&#x20;

Since the times of [Nikolay Lobachevsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolay_Lobachevsky), who pioneered the [non-Euclidean geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Euclidean_geometry), and [Pafnuty Chebyshev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pafnuty_Chebyshev), a prominent tutor, Russian [mathematicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Russian_mathematicians) became among the world's most influential.[^cite_note-math-507] [Dmitry Mendeleev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Mendeleev) invented the [Periodic table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periodic_table), the main framework of modern [chemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemistry).[^cite_note-508] Nine Soviet and Russian mathematicians have been awarded with the [Fields Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fields_Medal_winners). [Grigori Perelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigori_Perelman) was offered the first ever Clay [Millennium Prize Problems](27599234.md) Award for his final proof of the [Poincaré conjecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poincar%C3%A9_conjecture) in 2002, as well as the Fields Medal in 2006.[^cite_note-509]&#x20;



[Mikhail Lomonosov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Lomonosov) (1711–1765), [polymath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymath) scientist, inventor, poet and artist

[Alexander Popov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Stepanovich_Popov) was among the [inventors of radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invention_of_radio),[^cite_note-510] while [Nikolai Basov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Basov) and [Alexander Prokhorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Prokhorov) were co-inventors of [laser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser) and [maser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maser).[^cite_note-511] [Oleg Losev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleg_Losev) made crucial contributions in the field of [semiconductor junctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor_junction), and discovered [light-emitting diodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light-emitting_diode).[^cite_note-512] [Vladimir Vernadsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Vernadsky) is considered one of the founders of [geochemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geochemistry), [biogeochemistry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biogeochemistry), and [radiogeology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiometric_dating).[^cite_note-513] [Élie Metchnikoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lie_Metchnikoff) is known for his groundbreaking research in [immunology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immunology).[^cite_note-514] [Ivan Pavlov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Pavlov) is known chiefly for his work in [classical conditioning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_conditioning).[^cite_note-515] [Lev Landau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lev_Landau) made fundamental contributions to many areas of [theoretical physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theoretical_physics).[^cite_note-516]&#x20;

[Nikolai Vavilov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Vavilov) was best known for having identified the [centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vavilov_center) of origin of [cultivated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horticulture) plants.[^cite_note-517] [Trofim Lysenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trofim_Lysenko) was known mainly for [Lysenkoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lysenkoism).[^cite_note-518] Many famous Russian scientists and inventors were [émigrés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89migr%C3%A9s). [Igor Sikorsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Sikorsky) was an [aviation pioneer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_aviation_pioneers).[^cite_note-519] [Vladimir Zworykin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Zworykin) was the inventor of the [iconoscope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iconoscope) and [kinescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinescope) television systems.[^cite_note-520] [Theodosius Dobzhansky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodosius_Dobzhansky) was the central figure in the field of [evolutionary biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolutionary_biology) for his work in shaping the [modern synthesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_synthesis_\(20th_century\)).[^cite_note-521] [George Gamow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Gamow) was one of the foremost advocates of the [Big Bang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bang) theory.[^cite_note-522]&#x20;

#### Space exploration



[Mir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir), Russian [space station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_station) that operated in [LEO](47568.md)

[Roscosmos](406573.md) is Russia's national space agency. The country's achievements in the field of [space technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_technology) and [space exploration](28431.md) can be traced back to [Konstantin Tsiolkovsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Tsiolkovsky), the father of theoretical [astronautics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astronautics), whose works had inspired leading Soviet rocket engineers, such as [Sergey Korolyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Korolyov), [Valentin Glushko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentin_Glushko), and many others who contributed to the success of the [Soviet space programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_space_program) in the early stages of the [Space Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Race) and beyond.[^cite_note-523]<sup>: 6–7, 333 </sup>&#x20;

In 1957, the first Earth-orbiting artificial [satellite](27683.md), *[Sputnik 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sputnik_1)*, was launched. In 1961, the first human trip into space was successfully made by [Yuri Gagarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuri_Gagarin). Many other Soviet and Russian [space exploration records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_exploration_records) ensued. In 1963, [Valentina Tereshkova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentina_Tereshkova) became the first and youngest [woman in space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_space), having flown a solo mission on [Vostok 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vostok_6).[^cite_note-524] In 1965, [Alexei Leonov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexei_Leonov) became the first human to conduct a [spacewalk](9792.md), exiting the [space capsule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_capsule) during [Voskhod 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voskhod_2).[^cite_note-525]&#x20;

In 1957, [Laika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laika), a [Soviet space dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_space_dogs), became the first animal to orbit the Earth, aboard [Sputnik 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sputnik_2).[^cite_note-526] In 1966, [Luna 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_9) became the first spacecraft to achieve a survivable landing on a [celestial body](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astronomical_object), the [Moon](19331.md).[^cite_note-527] In 1968, [Zond 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zond_5) brought the first Earthlings (two tortoises and other life forms) to circumnavigate the Moon.[^cite_note-528] In 1970, [Venera 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venera_7) became the first spacecraft to land on another planet, [Venus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venus).[^cite_note-529] In 1971, [Mars 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mars_3) became the first spacecraft to land on [Mars](14640471.md).[^cite_note-530]<sup>: 34–60 </sup> During the same period, *[Lunokhod 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunokhod-1)* became the first [space exploration rover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_exploration_rover),[^cite_note-531] while *[Salyut 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salyut_1)* became the world's first [space station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_station).[^cite_note-532]&#x20;

As of 2023, Russia has 181 active satellites in space, which is the third-highest in the world.[^cite_note-533] Between the final flight of the [Space Shuttle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle) programme in 2011 and the 2020 [SpaceX](832774.md)'s [first crewed mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crew_Dragon_Demo-2), [Soyuz rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soyuz_\(rocket_family\)) were the only launch vehicles capable of transporting astronauts to the [ISS](15043.md).[^cite_note-534] [Luna 25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_25) launched in August 2023, was the first of the [Luna-Glob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna-Glob) Moon exploration programme.[^cite_note-535]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Russia](159643.md)



[Peterhof Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterhof_Palace) in Saint Petersburg, a UNESCO [World Heritage Site](44940.md)

Most foreign tourists come from China.[^cite_note-536] Major tourist routes in Russia include a journey around the [Golden Ring of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Ring_of_Russia), a [theme route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_route) of ancient Russian cities; cruises on large rivers such as the Volga; hikes on mountain ranges such as the [Caucasus Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caucasus_Mountains),[^cite_note-537] and journeys on the famous [Trans-Siberian Railway](56912.md).[^cite_note-538] Russia's most visited and popular landmarks include [Red Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Square), the [Peterhof Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterhof_Palace), the [Kazan Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan_Kremlin), the [Trinity Lavra of St. Sergius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_Lavra_of_St._Sergius) and Lake Baikal.[^cite_note-539]&#x20;

Moscow, the nation's cosmopolitan capital and historic core, is a [megacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megacity); it retains classical and Soviet-era architecture while boasting high art, world class ballet, and [modern skyscrapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_International_Business_Center).[^cite_note-540] [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md), the imperial capital, is famous for its classical architecture, cathedrals, museums and theatres, [white nights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Nights_Festival), crisscrossing rivers and numerous canals.[^cite_note-541] Russia is famed worldwide for its rich museums, such as the [State Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Museum), the [State Hermitage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermitage_Museum), and the [Tretyakov Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tretyakov_Gallery), and for theatres such as the [Bolshoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshoi_Theatre) and the [Mariinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariinsky_Theatre). The [Moscow Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_Kremlin) and the [Saint Basil's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Basil%27s_Cathedral) are among the cultural landmarks of Russia.[^cite_note-542]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Russia), [Russians](59510.md), [List of cities and towns in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Russia), and [List of cities and towns in Russia by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Russia_by_population)



Population density of Russian municipalities according to the 2021 census



Ethnic groups in Russia with a population of over one million according to the 2010 census

Russia had an estimated population of 146.0 million in 2025 (143.6 million excluding Crimea and Sevastopol),[^cite_note-gks.ru-popul-9] down from 147.2 million in the [2021 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Russian_census).[^cite_note-2021census-543] It is the [most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_population) in Europe and [ninth-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population) in the world. With a [population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_density) of 8.5 inhabitants per square kilometre (22 inhabitants/mi<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-544] Russia is one of the world's [most sparsely populated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density) countries,[^cite_note-cia-258] with the vast majority of its people concentrated within its [western part](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Russia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998153–166Population-545] The country is [highly urbanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization_by_country), with three-fourths of the population living in [urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Russia_by_population).[^cite_note-bite-251] As of 2025, the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) across Russia is estimated to be 1.37 children born per woman,[^cite_note-546] which is below the [replacement rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replacement_rate) of 2.1 and among [the lowest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependencies_by_total_fertility_rate).[^cite_note-547] Consequently, it has one of the [oldest populations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_median_age) in the world, with a median age of 41.9 years.[^cite_note-cia-258]&#x20;

Russia's population peaked at over 148 million in 1993, and subsequently declined due to its [death rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_rate) exceeding its [birth rate](515898.md), which some analysts have called a [demographic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_crisis_of_Russia).[^cite_note-548] In 2009, it recorded annual population growth for the first time in fifteen years, and subsequently experienced annual population growth due to declining [death rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_rates), increased [birth rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_rates), and increased immigration.[^cite_note-549] However, these population gains have been reversed since 2020, as excessive deaths from the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Russia) resulted in the largest peacetime decline in its history.[^cite_note-550] Following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), the demographic crisis has deepened,[^cite_note-551] owing to high military fatalities[^cite_note-552] and [renewed emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_emigration_during_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-553]&#x20;

Russia is a [multinational state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_state) with many subnational entities associated with different minorities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998172–201Ethnic_Composition-554] There are over [193 ethnic groups nationwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Russia). In the 2010 census, roughly 81% of the population were ethnic [Russians](59510.md), and the remaining 19% of the population were ethnic minorities.[^cite_note-555] Over four-fifths of Russia's population was of [European descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_of_Europe)—of whom the vast majority were [Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavs),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurtis1998172–201Ethnic_Composition-554][^cite_note-556] with a substantial minority of [Finno-Ugric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finno-Ugric_peoples) and [Germanic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germanic_peoples).[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558] Russia has the third-largest [immigrant population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Russia) in the world, with over 12 million immigrants residing in the country as of 2019.[^cite_note-559] The vast majority of the immigrants hail from [post-Soviet states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-Soviet_states), with about half of them being from [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainians_in_Russia) and [Kazakhstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazakhs_in_Russia) as of 2020.[^cite_note-560]&#x20;
